| **[Moment-Video: Diagnosing Temporal Fidelity of Video MLLMs on Momentary Visual Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02522v3)** | 2026-11-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have made rapid progress in general and long-form video understanding, yet their ability to capture and use brief, answer-critical visual evidence remains underexplored. Momentary actions or state transitions may determine an answer while occupying only a small fraction of the video. Performance on broad video understanding tasks alone does not establish whether models can reliably identify and use this temporally localized evidence. We introduce Moment-Video, a benchmark for evaluating temporal fidelity through momentary visual event understanding. Questions are constructed around visually observable events or event sequences, requiring models to notice, count, describe, or reason about transient evidence. The benchmark contains 1,000 human-verified video-QA pairs across seven domains and 25 subcategories, covering four tasks: Temporal Occurrence, Temporal Counting, Action Description, and Temporal Reasoning. We evaluate 34 proprietary and open-source MLLMs. Under the main evaluation settings, the best-performing model, Seed-2.0-Pro, achieves 39.6% overall accuracy, while most open-source models remain below 25%. Diagnostic analyses show that denser frame sampling improves some models, but substantial errors persist in an audited subset even when human review judges the sampled visual evidence sufficient to answer. Performance also declines on longer videos, suggesting additional temporal-localization challenges. By exposing these limitations, Moment-Video provides a diagnostic benchmark to guide the evaluation and development of video MLLMs that more reliably understand brief, answer-critical visual events.</p></details> | <details><summary>36 pa...</summary><p>36 pages, 12 figures, 20 tables</p></details> |
| **[EVIDENT: Routing MLLM Adaptation through Entity-Grounded Visual Evidence for Cross-Domain Video Temporal Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26104v3)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Fine-tuning MLLMs for Video Temporal Grounding (VTG) often improves in-domain performance but degrades sharply under domain shift. In this work, we find that this failure is primarily driven not just by unseen query concepts, but by visual domain shift, which prevents the model from coupling its learned temporal localization knowledge with its inherent entity-attention capability. To address this, we introduce EVIDENT, a parameter-efficient adaptation framework that anchors temporal grounding in the inherent entity-attention of pre-trained MLLMs by routing VTG adaptation through explicit visual entity evidence. EVIDENT consists of three components: (i) an Entity Bottleneck Adapter that transforms dense visual tokens into compact entity-level slots, (ii) an Entity-Binding Distillation loss that instills objectness priors into the semantically unstructured MLLM visual space, guiding each slot to bind to a coherent entity, and (iii) an Entity-to-eVidence gating mechanism that leverages the captured entities as evidence, steering the model to localize moments containing query-relevant entities. Together, these components enable VTG fine-tuning to rely on entity-grounded evidence rather than brittle dataset shortcuts. Experiments on cross-domain VTG benchmarks show that EVIDENT consistently improves out-of-domain robustness while preserving competitive in-domain performance with modest parameter overhead. These results suggest that entity-level grounding is an effective inductive bias for generalizable temporal localization.</p></details> | <details><summary>NeurI...</summary><p>NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://mreraser.github.io/EVIDENT_project_page/</p></details> |
| **[SPACE: Source-free Proxy Anchor Concept Erasure for MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09868v2)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>As Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) face growing privacy risks and regulatory constraints, machine unlearning (MU) has emerged as a crucial solution for removing sensitive data while preserving model performance. However, existing MU methods typically rely on visual data of the target concepts, which is often unavailable due to strict data retention policies, thus creating a demand for source-free unlearning approaches that operate without access to the target data. In this work, we propose Source-free Proxy Anchor Concept Erasure (SPACE), the first source-free unlearning framework specialized for MLLMs. SPACE consists of two stages: (1) Text-Guided Proxy Anchor Selection (TPAS), which retrieves semantically aligned proxy anchors from the shared feature space. (2) Dual-Constraint Semantic Isolation (DCSI), which optimizes these anchors to indirectly erase target concepts. DCSI confines updates to the null space of retained knowledge, ensuring structural integrity. We theoretically prove that SPACE strictly bounds the perturbation on retained knowledge and maximizes feature spectral entropy, thereby maintaining the model's performance. Furthermore, extensive experiments across six datasets show that SPACE achieves performance comparable to that of state-of-the-art data-dependent methods, validating its effectiveness in source-free MU scenarios. The source code will be released.</p></details> |  |
| **[WM^MLLM: Turning MLLMs into World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24193v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video world models keep the world in the context of a generator, so a revisit is synthesized again and earlier edits can drift or disappear. We present WM^MLLM, the first interactive world model built on multimodal large language models (MLLMs), for persistent, editable world generation from a single image. WM^MLLM keeps the world state outside every generator, as a panorama with a revision history, and lets the MLLMs write it: they turn each request into operations on this state, select where a local edit is written, and check every candidate before it becomes the next state. Image generators only propose views, and video only presents them. Users can walk to new places and return, edit objects through text or a box, place subjects, and change lighting, weather or season. By construction, a revisit at the same revision reproduces the earlier view exactly, a local edit leaves every panorama pixel outside its region unchanged, and each request starts from the result of the previous one. Against six world model baselines on MBench-A, WM^MLLM has the highest mean on all four environment-recovery metrics (Epipolar 91.5 versus 0.1 to 74.0) and completes the most camera round trips (85.3% versus at most 50.9%). It has the best value in all 15 CompBench columns against 15 baselines. On 112 local edits judged blind, selecting the region to write after generation raises completion from 34.8% to 83.0%. On 258 WBench and MBench-A items, all 46 revisits recover their earlier views, and all 97 local edits that pass the check leave panorama pixels outside their region unchanged.</p></details> | <details><summary>25 pa...</summary><p>25 pages, 14 figures, 8 tables; project page: https://ztmotalee.github.io/wmmllm/</p></details> |
| **[Multimodal Information Fusion for Chart Understanding: A Survey of MLLMs -- Evolution, Limitations, and Cognitive Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10138v2)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Chart understanding is a quintessential information fusion task, requiring the seamless integration of graphical and textual data to extract meaning. The advent of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) has revolutionized this domain, yet the landscape of MLLM-based chart analysis remains fragmented and lacks systematic organization. This survey provides a comprehensive roadmap of this nascent frontier by structuring the domain's core components. We begin by analyzing the fundamental challenges of fusing visual and linguistic information in charts. We then categorize downstream tasks and datasets, introducing a novel taxonomy of canonical and non-canonical benchmarks to highlight the field's expanding scope. Subsequently, we present a comprehensive evolution of methodologies, tracing the progression from classic deep learning techniques to state-of-the-art MLLM paradigms that leverage sophisticated fusion strategies. By critically examining the limitations of current models, particularly their perceptual and reasoning deficits, we identify promising future directions, including advanced alignment techniques and reinforcement learning for cognitive enhancement. This survey aims to equip researchers and practitioners with a structured understanding of how MLLMs are transforming chart information fusion and to catalyze progress toward more robust and reliable systems.</p></details> |  |
| **[Scenes as an Attack Surface: Uncovering Scene-Grounded Safety Failures in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14094v2)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing jailbreak attacks have exposed serious safety weaknesses in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Most prior attacks rely on specially constructed visual inputs to carry or support harmful intent. In this paper, we uncover a different vulnerability: task-relevant scene semantics alone can shift an MLLM from refusal to compliance, even when the visual input introduces no additional attack instructions and the original task intent remains unchanged. We formalize this failure as a scene-grounded visual safety vulnerability and introduce SceneSafetyBench to investigate it across eight risk categories and nine white- and black-box MLLMs. Our analysis reveals that this failure is associated with how task-relevant visual evidence influences downstream safety representations, suggesting that visual grounding alone cannot fully explain the observed safety shift. Building on this finding, we propose Scene-Grounded Attacks (SGA), which preserve the original task while constructing validated scene-grounded queries and combining their independent responses. Across nine target MLLMs, SGA achieves an average attack success rate (ASR) of 86.17%, outperforming the strongest baseline by 8.55 percentage points and achieving the highest ASR on eight models. Our results identify scene semantics as an overlooked attack surface in MLLMs and highlight a growing tension between stronger visual grounding and robust multimodal safety alignment.</p></details> | <details><summary>The a...</summary><p>The authors have identified limitations in the mechanistic analysis presented in this version. In particular, the current evidence is insufficient to fully substantiate the proposed explanation of scene-grounded safety failures. We are withdrawing this version to prevent potentially overstated conclusions</p></details> |
| **[SpecLook: Speculating Where to Look for Fast and Reliable Agentic MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23483v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Agentic multimodal large language models (MLLMs) that think with images interleave reasoning with zoom-in tool calls. However, this sequential loop is slow, and its answers carry no reliability score. Prior speculation of tokens, reasoning steps, or answers keeps the loop, and answer speculation also accepts confident errors. We find that the loop mostly adds one zoom-in step, with which a small model is ahead of the agent on most splits. Our key insight is to speculate the agent's next observation, which, unlike a drafted answer, can be verified, corrected, and used to test the answer. We propose SpecLook, a training-free speculate-and-verify framework with three levels. A small model drafts a glimpse and re-answers from it, and the question ends if the answer is unchanged and confident. Otherwise, the agent's large model verifies and corrects the glimpse without tools, and their answers are fused. Only questions that still lack evidence reach the agent, in one forward pass that SpecLook-Fast skips, so no question runs the loop. The trace scores every answer's reliability without labels. Extensive experiments on V*, HR-Bench, and POPE show that both are more accurate than every baseline on every split. SpecLook reaches 87.2% and 86.2% mean accuracy with 7.7x and 4.7x speedups over the DeepEyes-7B and Thyme-RL agents, and SpecLook-Fast reaches 9.9x and 6.3x.</p></details> | Technical report |
| **[VIVID: Beyond Static Multi-Image Reasoning with Localized Visual Differences and Textual Entity Dynamics in Video MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23153v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video question answering under tight frame budgets requires preserving localized motion and event order, yet current video multimodal large language models (MLLMs) reduce comprehension to static multi-image reasoning by discarding unselected frames and encoding keyframes independently: (1) Spatially, whole-frame pooling dilutes small moving targets into static backgrounds; (2) Temporally, dropping intermediate frames or averaging tokens erases inter-frame motion, confusing direction, speed, and order. We propose VIVID (Visual Inter-frame Variations and Individual Dynamics), which moves beyond static snapshots like human vision: keyframes capture core states while inter-frame Patch Diffs preserve neighboring changes within $K_f$ keyframes. Autonomous Delta-Driven Keyframe Planning (ADKP) selects anchors from a $3\text{ fps}$ pool and feeds surrounding patch differences into Dual-Road Change Memory (DRCM), forming Dynamic Tokens at zero extra anchor tokens alongside a camera-stabilized Active Motion Region of Interest (ROI) and chronological Entity State Chains. Adding $0.22\%$ trainable parameters to a frozen $27\text{B}$ backbone, VIVID-Small ($50$ examples, $\sim 1.5$ GPU hours) beats all eleven baselines in overall accuracy across $8,083$ questions and trails only VIVID-Large ($3,600$ examples), which leads on MotionBench, Video-MME, and TempCompass.</p></details> | <details><summary>14 pa...</summary><p>14 pages, 2 figures, 5 tables. Code: https://github.com/step-out/VIVID</p></details> |
| **[Visual Contextual Attack: Jailbreaking MLLMs with Image-Driven Context Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02844v3)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>With the emergence of strong vision language capabilities, multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated tremendous potential for real-world applications. However, the security vulnerabilities exhibited by the visual modality pose significant challenges to deploying such models in open-world environments. Recent studies have successfully induced harmful responses from target MLLMs by encoding harmful textual semantics directly into visual inputs. However, in these approaches, the visual modality primarily serves as a trigger for unsafe behavior, often exhibiting semantic ambiguity and lacking grounding in realistic scenarios. In this work, we define a novel setting: vision-centric jailbreak, where visual information serves as a necessary component in constructing a complete and realistic jailbreak context. Building on this setting, we propose the VisCo (Visual Contextual) Attack. VisCo fabricates contextual dialogue using four distinct vision-focused strategies, dynamically generating auxiliary images when necessary to construct a vision-centric jailbreak scenario. To maximize attack effectiveness, it incorporates automatic toxicity obfuscation and semantic refinement to produce a final attack prompt that reliably triggers harmful responses from the target black-box MLLMs. Specifically, VisCo achieves a toxicity score of 4.78 and an Attack Success Rate (ASR) of 85% on MM-SafetyBench against GPT-4o, significantly outperforming the baseline, which achieves a toxicity score of 2.48 and an ASR of 22.2%. Code: https://github.com/Dtc7w3PQ/Visco-Attack.</p></details> |  |
| **[Diagnosing and Correcting Bias in MLLM for Long Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36210v2)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Long-video question answering is challenging since the answer often hinges on a few decisive moments scattered throughout the video, while memory constraints force Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to sample frames at extremely low rates, resulting in sparsity that risks omitting key evidence. Though aggressively enlarging the context size is intuitive, we argue that performance remains fundamentally constrained by the bias, i.e., the misleading cues caused by spurious linguistic correlations and salient yet irrelevant visual observations. In this paper, we first introduce S-MME, a benchmark designed to systematically diagnose shortcut-biased behavior in MLLMs. To mitigate this failure, we further propose a training-free framework, Counterfactual Long-video Evidence-Aware Reasoning (CLEAR). By treating the full video-question pair as a complete view, CLEAR estimates bias effects via augmented views by only keeping the question or locally salient visual clips. Then, we intervene hidden states to mitigate the discrepancy at inference such that the correct answer can be predicted confidently. With experimental studies across five benchmarks, we show that our method is generalizable and can be integrated in different MLLMs, yielding consistent gains in prediction and robustness. We hope CLEAR can offer a complementary direction in test-time scaling for long-video understanding.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted at NeurIPS 2026, with acknowledgent details updated. Code:https://github.com/Xiaoqiovo/CLEAR</p></details> |
| **[OmniTongue: A Comprehensive MLLM Benchmark for Automated Tongue Diagnosis in Traditional Chinese Medicine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22652v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have excelled in Western medical imaging, yet their potential in Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) tongue diagnosis remains systematically unevaluated. Tongue diagnosis requires sophisticated global and local perception, serving as a rigorous touchstone for assessing the visual understanding depth of MLLMs. However, existing datasets are limited by single dimensions, coarse granularity, and ambiguous labels, hindering the evaluation of authentic clinical capabilities. To bridge this gap, we introduce OmniTongue, the first comprehensive, fine-grained Visual Question Answering benchmark for TCM tongue diagnosis. This benchmark integrates 1,351 expert-validated images and 21,616 instruction pairs. Its core design features 16 clinical dimensions for multidimensional evaluation, region-specific annotations for pathological localization, and 66 fine-grained labels for precise diagnostic representation. Extensive evaluations of existing MLLMs reveal significant performance gaps, demonstrating that medical-specific models lack domain advantages, while universally poor Macro-F1 scores expose a reliance on statistical shortcuts rather than genuine understanding of tongue pathology. These findings not only highlight the current limitations of MLLMs in interpreting regional medical features but also underscore OmniTongue's significance for developing robust automated tongue diagnosis models. The code and dataset will be made publicly available in a future release.</p></details> | <details><summary>11 pa...</summary><p>11 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables</p></details> |
| **[From Perception to Simulation: Unveiling and Empowering Mental Spatial Dynamics in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22541v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Spatial reasoning is a fundamental component of world understanding for Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). While existing research has advanced the perception of spatial relations in static scenes, the domain of dynamic spatial simulation remains relatively unexplored. To bridge this gap, we introduce MentalGrid, a benchmark focused on spatial reasoning tasks that require models to simulate evolving spatial states without intermediate visual feedback. Extensive evaluation on our benchmark reveals a significant deficiency in the spatial simulation capabilities of existing models. Specifically, we find that performance is constrained by perceptual limitations in simple simulation scenarios and hindered by inadequate multi-step reasoning as complexity increases. To address these challenges, we propose a three-stage curriculum learning framework utilizing 16k specialized spatial reasoning samples. This approach yields SpatialWalker, which achieves a 33.8% average improvement over its base model, surpassing proprietary models like Gemini-3-Flash in spatial simulation. Furthermore, SpatialWalker demonstrates robust generalization, maintaining a 4.9% average gain on out-of-domain benchmarks, demonstrating the pivotal role of spatial simulation as a fundamental building block for broader spatial intelligence.</p></details> | <details><summary>Findi...</summary><p>Findings of EMNLP 2026</p></details> |
| **[Dressed in Authority: Practical Black-Box Jailbreaking of MLLMs via Visual Trust Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22292v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are rapidly advancing, yet their safety remains a critical concern, especially under jailbreak attempts. Existing MLLM jailbreaks either inject fragile adversarial perturbations into irrelevant images or synthesize toxic visual cues to achieve text-driven attack intents. In contrast, a more practical MLLM jailbreak emphasizes enhancing attack efficacy directly on naturally occurring harmful image-text pairs. We address this problem by proposing Visual Trust Bias Attack (VTBA), a new jailbreak framework that exploits a previously overlooked attack surface: MLLMs exhibit an intrinsic trust bias toward authoritative visual features, which enables transferable and stealthy jailbreaks. VTBA constructs a bank of authoritative templates, matches each input pair to the most relevant one, and integrates the harmful intent into the selected layout, thereby leveraging credible visual cues to override safety refusals while preserving naturalness. We further construct a dataset of 2,500 naturally occurring image-text pairs to evaluate jailbreaks in this practical multimodal setting. Extensive experiments show that our method consistently achieves high attack success rates across strong MLLMs, revealing a severe and previously underexplored weakness in current multimodal safety alignment. Warning: This paper may contain sensitive content.</p></details> | 10 pages, 9 figures |
| **[SARVIA: Fast-Adapting the Strong SAR Foundation Models That Are Already in Your MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22273v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Synthetic aperture radar (SAR) foundation models provide transferable representations for diverse perception tasks in all-weather Earth observation. However, their development often relies on costly large-scale SAR pretraining, raising the question of whether such extensive domain-specific pretraining is necessary. We find that modern multimodal large language models (MLLMs) already provide visual encoders that can outperform dedicated SAR foundation models as downstream initializations. This motivates a new paradigm: reuse general visual capabilities and devote domain training to brief specialization. We therefore introduce SARVIA, an adaptive post-pretraining method that specializes modern MLLMs using self-supervised visual questions with computable answers. These questions are constructed from unlabeled observations through general image tasks that capture spatial relations and SAR-specific tasks that exploit native measurements and SAR--optical correspondence. A shared answer-prediction objective jointly trains low-rank adapters across visual and language components, with loss-driven task sampling. We then export the adapted visual encoder as a standalone backbone. With 600 adaptation steps and 2 GPU-hours, SARVIA outperforms the compared foundation models on all six SAR detection and segmentation benchmarks. Compared with dedicated SAR pretraining, SARVIA reduces domain-training costs by orders of magnitude while improving visual transfer across architectures and scales. This new paradigm shifts the emphasis from repeated, costly pretraining to a reusable specialization process that can benefit from advances in open MLLMs. Dataset and code: https://github.com/zcablii/SARVIA.</p></details> |  |
| **[TIVS: Mapping the Sensitivity Landscape of Visual Token Transformations in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19745v2)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent efforts to improve the efficiency of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have explored visual token pruning, quantization, and feature compression. However, the properties that determine whether transformed visual tokens remain usable by a frozen decoder are not well characterized. We introduce \textbf{TIVS (Token Identity Versus Statistics)}, a training-free intervention framework that separately studies sequence permutation, token retention, numerical precision, and statistical replacement under controlled conditions. On 192 POPE questions, three token permutations change TinyLLaVA accuracy by only $-0.17$ percentage points on average, while changing 4.69\% of individual predictions. Diagonal and rank-64 Gaussian replacements decrease accuracy by 25.0 and 28.1 points on the same subset. Further experiments with exact moment constraints, quantization error, token retention, and additional visual tasks reveal how strongly the effects depend on the original feature values and the evaluation setting. These findings provide an empirical basis for assessing visual token transformations in frozen MLLMs and designing more reliable multimodal interfaces.</p></details> | <details><summary>9 pag...</summary><p>9 pages, 12 figures, 3 tables</p></details> |
| **[Beyond Token Stacking: Multi-scale Visual Modulation and Adaptive Fused-feature Calibration for MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21168v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have advanced rapidly and follow two main paradigms: one involves concatenating visual and textual tokens for input into Large Language Model (LLM), while the other injects visual knowledge into Feed-Forward Networks (FFNs) of the LLM. However, the former introduces a large number of redundant visual tokens, substantially increasing computational overhead. The latter reduces sequence length but removes images from the input stage entirely, which may hinder the activation of image-related knowledge. Moreover, naively injecting visual knowledge into the FFNs could induce activation distribution shifts due to cross-modal semantic discrepancies. To address these challenges, we develop Beyond Token Stacking (BTS), a novel visual enhancement framework for MLLMs. Specifically, we propose a Multi-scale Visual Modulation (MVM) mechanism that injects multi-scale visual information at the input stage via modulation without increasing the token sequence length. Furthermore, we develop an Adaptive Fused-feature Calibration (AFC) module that performs per-channel affine calibration to decouple visual injection from distribution alignment in the FFN layers, improving multimodal fusion stability while preserving language priors. Experiments show that our approach achieves state-of-the-art performance.</p></details> |  |
| **[MemDoctor: Stage-wise Error Attribution and Repair for MLLM Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21019v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) now assist users across sessions of images and text, carried by an external memory of what was said and shown. When such an assistant answers wrongly, the failure usually began earlier, in how a record was written, updated or retrieved, and the answer alone does not say where. We formalize multimodal memory as four stages, Write, Update, Retrieve and Answer, and define nine error types across them. A stage-resolved analysis on Mem-Gallery traces most failures to a source stage, and most of those lie before the answer. Injecting known errors against fact-preserving control edits, we then follow each tainted record downstream. Early errors survive to the answer as apparent evidence, and hurt most when no other record holds the fact. A diagnosis that trusts the quoted evidence therefore blames the stage where the error surfaces, not the one where it began. We propose MemDoctor, in which the MLLM that built the memory diagnoses its own failures. It traces each needed fact backward through the four stages, accepts a diagnosis only when the cited evidence is found in memory, and repairs that stage before answering again. On a 4B backbone, MemDoctor improves accuracy on both Mem-Gallery and MemLens, outperforming all evaluated 8B baselines, and the same loop also improves MuRAG and A-Mem.</p></details> |  |
| **[ProSE: Probing Reconstruction Error for Semantic-guided Expert Skipping in MoE-MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20885v1)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Mixture-of-Experts Multimodal Large Language Models (MoE-MLLMs) offer scalable inference through sparse expert activation, yet substantial computational redundancy remains among the activated experts. Existing expert skipping methods typically calibrate thresholds based on target skipping ratios or final-output divergence, overlooking the accumulation of skipping-induced error across layers and the varying semantic importance of individual tokens. To address that, we propose ProSE, a training-free, semantic-guided expert skipping framework that probes layer-wise reconstruction error to promote representation consistency compared with default activation. Specifically, we construct query-aware semantic masks to identify salient visual tokens and progressively calibrate modality-specific skipping thresholds for each MoE layer. By minimizing masked reconstruction error under a given budget, ProSE maintains semantic consistency after skipping and allows adaptive expert allocation under limited activation budgets. Extensive experiments on 9 benchmarks demonstrate consistent improvements on average over existing expert skipping methods, particularly under aggressive skipping settings. ProSE maintains over 96\% performance on KimiVL-A3B-Inst with 83\% expert skipping and accelerates prefilling by $1.7\times$.</p></details> | 8 pages |
| **[MI-Pruner: Crossmodal Mutual Information-guided Token Pruner for Efficient MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03072v2)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The redundancy of visual tokens in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) motivates training-free token pruning techniques for efficient inference. Existing methods typically rank visual tokens by attention salience and retain only the most salient ones. However, salience-based pruning is susceptible to sink tokens and positional bias, overlooking the diversity of the selected set. Moreover, extracting attention maps incurs additional overhead. In this paper, we propose MI-Pruner, which derives Pointwise Mutual Information (PMI) based relevance scores in the projection space to reflect crossmodal relevance and intra-modal redundancy. Grounded in information theory, our approach identifies the most informative tokens by explicitly measuring feature-level dependency. Without reliance on internal attention maps or architectural modifications, MI-Pruner can be seamlessly applied to any pretrained MLLMs for inference acceleration. Extensive experiments on LLaVA1.5 and Qwen-series demonstrate that our approach achieves a favorable performance-efficiency trade-off. Specifically, we preserve ${>}97\%$ performance on LLaVA1.5-7B at a pruning rate of 88.9\%.</p></details> | 9 pages |
| **[GRASP: Guided Region-Aware Sparse Prompting for Adapting MLLMs to Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17089v2)** | 2026-11-14 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In recent years, Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have made significant progress in visual question answering tasks. However, directly applying existing fine-tuning methods to remote sensing (RS) images often leads to issues such as overfitting on background noise or neglecting target details. This is primarily due to the large-scale variations, sparse target distributions, and complex regional semantic features inherent in RS images. These challenges limit the effectiveness of MLLMs in RS tasks. To address these challenges, we propose a parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) strategy called Guided Region-Aware Sparse Prompting (GRASP). GRASP introduces spatially structured soft prompts associated with spatial blocks extracted from a frozen visual token grid. Through a question-guided sparse fusion mechanism, GRASP dynamically aggregates task-specific context into a compact global prompt, enabling the model to focus on relevant regions while filtering out background noise. Extensive experiments on multiple RSVQA benchmarks show that GRASP achieves competitive performance compared to existing fine-tuning and prompt-based methods while maintaining high parameter efficiency.</p></details> |  |
| **[XLSAR-Bench: Towards Extremely Large SAR Imagery Understanding of MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19364v1)** | 2026-11-14 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The rapid development of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) has highlighted the need for comprehensive benchmarks to assess their capabilities and limitations in remote sensing (RS). However, evaluating MLLMs in large-size synthetic aperture radar (SAR) scenarios remains challenging, as understanding complete scenes requires both global semantic interpretation and fine-grained target perception. Existing SAR benchmarks predominantly rely on small image patches, limiting their ability to assess these capabilities jointly. To address these limitations, we present XLSAR-Bench, a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating the perception and reasoning capabilities of MLLMs in large-scene SAR imagery. XLSAR-Bench comprises 405 images with an average size of 7153$\times$7153 pixels, ten tasks spanning visual question answering, visual grounding, and detailed image captioning, with all evaluation samples meticulously annotated manually, assisted by a semi-automatic captioner. Experimental results from general-purpose and domain-specific MLLMs reveal persistent challenges in understanding large SAR scenes and accurately perceiving small targets. We have open-sourced XLSAR-Bench to support further research in developing more powerful MLLMs for complete large SAR scenes.</p></details> |  |
| **[Generate, Align, and Restore: MLLM-Empowered Diffusion for Generalizable Low-light Image Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19285v1)** | 2026-11-14 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Low-light image enhancement (LLIE) is essential to numerous vision applications operating under adverse illumination. Despite substantial advances, real-world nighttime enhancement remains challenging due to the scarcity of strictly aligned day-night pairs and complex degradations involving extreme darkness and heterogeneous artificial light sources, leading to fragile generalization. To overcome these challenges, we propose MrLIE (MLLM-empowered reference Low-light Image Enhancement), a novel reference-based unsupervised LLIE framework that synergizes the generative and reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) with conditional diffusion. MrLIE employs the MLLM as a high-fidelity reference synthesizer and diagnostic critic, iteratively generating, diagnosing, and correcting normal-light references directly from degraded nighttime observations. Based on this, our method governs the enhancement process through two complementary mechanisms, in which a confidence offset module rectifies spatial-geometric inconsistencies for reference reliability and a structure-anchored injection scheme harmonizes aligned reference cues, low-light structural constraints, and ControlNet additive anchoring during the denoising process. Extensive experiments on diverse benchmarks demonstrate that MrLIE achieves superior perceptual quality, natural appearance, and robust generalization.</p></details> | 14 pages, 13 figures |
| **[Can MLLMs Reason over Surround-View Driving Videos? In parallel, we propose an automated evaluation framework, ToxiEval, which integrates toxicity endpoint prediction, synthetic accessibility, drug-likeness, and structural similarity into a high-throughput evaluation chain for repair success. We systematically assess 43 mainstream general-purpose MLLMs and conduct multiple ablation studies to analyze key issues, including evaluation metrics, candidate diversity, and failure attribution. Experimental results show that although current MLLMs still face significant challenges on this task, they begin to demonstrate promising capabilities in toxicity understanding, semantic constraint adherence, and structure-aware editing.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted by KDD 2026 (AI for Sciences Track, Oral)</p></details> |
| **[Where to Connect? Boosting MLLMs via Dynamic Gated Pathways across ALL ViT and LLM Layers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06844v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>A central yet under-explored question in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) is where to connect---how visual information from a hierarchical vision encoder should be wired into the layer-wise semantics of a large language model. Recent multi-layer fusion methods either compress hierarchical features on the encoder side or rely on predefined sparse or hierarchical layer-to-layer connections, and therefore stop short of modeling adaptive cross-layer interactions between the ViT and LLM hierarchies. We address this gap with DGP (Dynamic Gated Pathways), a routing mechanism that establishes all-to-all connections between every ViT layer and every LLM layer, with each LLM layer dynamically gating its preference over visual layers conditioned on its own semantic state. Built on top of LLaVA-1.5-7B and 13B, DGP delivers consistent gains over the LLaVA-1.5-7B baseline (e.g., +4.2 on SQA$^{I}$, +3.3 on MM-Vet, +3.1 on VizWiz and LLaVA$^{W}$) and surpasses prior multi-layer visual fusion methods on most benchmarks. Accepted at the 40th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2026)</p></details> |

On a sketch, the sketch is the image, and text prompts detect, point, resolve object and spatial referring expressions, and ground affordances in it. | **[PI-MLLM: Psychophysics-Inspired Multimodal Large Language Model for Oddly Satisfying Video Emotion Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28264v1)** | 2026-10-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Oddly Satisfying Videos (OSVs) can evoke strong viewer responses through repetitive motion, visual tactility, and ASMR-like sounds despite containing few explicit semantic emotion cues. This makes OSVs a challenging setting for Video Emotion Analysis (VEA), since most existing methods focus on semantic video content and under-model the physical sensory dynamics that shape viewer responses. To support Oddly Satisfying Video Emotion Analysis (OSVEA), we construct the Oddly Satisfying Video Dataset (OSVD), comprising 12,000 annotated 5-second clips. Each clip provides viewer-emotion labels under video-only, audio-only, and audio-visual conditions, together with explanations grounded in visual and acoustic sensory evidence. Building on OSVD, we propose PI-MLLM, a psychophysics-inspired multimodal large language model that integrates Modality-Specific Dynamics Alignment (MSDA) and Coalition-Based Audio-Visual Attribution (CAVA). MSDA uses auxiliary prediction objectives to align modality representations with visual motion and acoustic energy-onset dynamics. CAVA evaluates emotion scores across modality coalitions and applies Shapley attribution to identify the dominant modality and determine whether visual and auditory evidence is synergistic or conflicting. Experiments show that PI-MLLM improves both viewer-side emotion prediction and sensory-grounded explanation quality, suggesting the value of physical sensory cues to video emotion analysis.</p></details> | <details><summary>12 pa...</summary><p>12 pages, 5 figures, 8 tables; includes supplementary material</p></details> |
| **[Beyond Omni: A Unified MLLM for Embodied Sensory Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26751v1)** | 2026-10-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent embodied systems increasingly build on multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for their pretrained understanding and reasoning capabilities, but their perception of the physical world remains largely limited to basic modalities such as vision and audio. However, broadening MLLMs to more diverse sensory modalities from the physical environment is challenging: their representations are often weakly connected to the MLLM and vary substantially in structure and statistics, while their interpretation still requires shared high-level understanding and reasoning. We therefore propose Unisense, a unified embodied MLLM framework that supports diverse physical sensory modalities within a shared multimodal architecture. Unisense follows a two-stage training process: (i) aligning sensory representations with language to establish a language-accessible interface; and (ii) tuning modality-specific experts within the shared LLM to provide specialized processing for different sensory modalities. To support broad sensory learning, we curate Unisense-1M, a large-scale embodied sensory training corpus comprising 1M samples across 8 sensory modalities. We further evaluate Unisense on 12 benchmarks spanning depth, point cloud, IMU, tactile, thermal, radar, motion, and smell understanding. Across these heterogeneous sensing tasks, Unisense achieves strong performance and outperforms both generalist and specialized models on most benchmarks, demonstrating the potential of pretrained MLLMs as unified models for embodied sensory understanding.</p></details> |  |

Existing Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) paradigms force models to mimic ground truth without modeling uncertainty, often resulting in blind guessing on tiny or unrecognizable objects in aerial scenes. Drawing inspiration from the reasoning-incentivizing mechanisms of DeepSeek-R1, we find that trustworthy perception stems from consistency across views, establishing a visual parallel to the Chain-of-Thought in logic. To effectively adapt policy optimization for non-autoregressive mask generation, we introduce Hybrid-View Group Advantage-Weighted Regression (H-GAWR). This strategy constructs dynamic groups of high-resolution and low-resolution views, seamlessly bridging group-relative advantage estimation with advantage-weighted policy extraction. By enforcing consensus on clear inputs and strictly penalizing hallucinations on ambiguous ones, Aerial-R1 develops an explicitly incentivized refusal mechanism. Extensive experiments on VSAI-Ref, the first aerial MLLM benchmark dedicated to jointly evaluating dense reasoning and hallucination robustness, demonstrate that Aerial-R1 significantly outperforms SFT baselines in segmentation precision while substantially mitigating fatal hallucinations in low-information scenarios, paving the way for more reliable and trustworthy aerial agents. | **[Can MLLMs Find Their Way in a City? Exploring Emergent Navigation from Web-Scale Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15933v4)** | 2026-10-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Leveraging multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to develop embodied agents offers significant promise for addressing complex real-world tasks. To address this, we propose Verbalization of Path(VoP), which explicitly grounds the agent's internal reasoning by probing city-scale cognitive maps (key landmarks and directions toward the destination) from the MLLM, substantially enhancing navigation success. Project Webpage: https://dwipddalal.github.io/AgentNav/</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted at EACL 2026 (ORAL)</p></details> |
| **[MLLM-Guided Autonomous Object Detection for Open-World Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.10844v2)** | 2026-10-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Object detection typically relies on predefined category vocabularies, while open-world and open-vocabulary approaches remain limited by generic unknown labels or externally specified detection concepts. | **[MobileInject: Evaluating Reasoning-to-Action Security of MLLM-Based Mobile Agents under Cross-Modal Consistency Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23307v1)** | 2026-10-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal large language model (MLLM)-based mobile agents are rapidly evolving from conversational assistants into autonomous systems that perceive, reason over, and act on smartphone environments. Although prompt injection has been studied in desktop and web settings, its cross-modal properties in mobile ecosystems remain poorly understood, and existing security analyses are still limited. To close this gap, we introduce MobileInject, the first benchmark for evaluating mobile agents against multimodal injection attacks and reasoning-to-action security risks. MobileInject provides a high-fidelity interactive mobile evaluation framework covering 11 scenarios, 9 security-risk categories, and 108 executable risk instances. It further introduces Cross-Modal Consistency Injection to exploit corroboration across modalities, two enhanced attack methods, Multi-Role Collaborative Deception and Reasoning-Camouflage Injection, to intensify trust manipulation and reasoning hijacking, and a Reasoning Security Diagnosis Framework for fine-grained failure analysis. Systematic experiments show that mobile agents are broadly vulnerable to multimodal injection, and that many failures stem from reasoning hijacking at early stages. TREC 2025 Workshop, Dec. 2025</p></details> |
| **[LDDR: Linear-DPP Dynamic-Resolution Frame Sampling for Video MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11477v2)** | 2026-10-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal large language models, when used for long video understanding, require selecting an informative frame set from massive and redundant candidates. | **[Harmful YouTube Video Detection: A Taxonomy of Online Harm and MLLMs as Alternative Annotators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.05854v2)** | 2026-10-14 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video platforms, used by billions globally, are criticized for exposing users to harmful content. | **[Beyond Static Cues: Unified Dynamic Detection of MLLM Backdoors via Dual-Flow Trajectory Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20319v1)** | 2026-10-14 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) are increasingly deployed in safety-critical domains, yet their susceptibility to backdoor attacks remains a formidable challenge. To capture this behavior, we propose EVO-Detect, a unified detection framework that monitors the dynamic evolution of internal signals during early decoding steps through two complementary channels: Attention Flow and Logits Flow. The attention branch measures how image-text attention allocation evolves during early generation, whereas the logits branch quantifies the sensitivity of the output distribution to modal perturbations through interventional ablations. We then convert these trajectories into compact sample-level metrics and normalize them against negative-control signatures so that natural perturbations are less likely to be mistaken for backdoor evidence. | **[VisualWorldBench: A Fine-Grained Multi-Task Benchmark for Evaluating Visual World Knowledge in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20083v1)** | 2026-10-14 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Visual world knowledge is a core capability of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), underpinning knowledge-intensive visual question answering and fine-grained visual reasoning. | **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[S-Mind: Internalizing Multi-View Geometry for Spatial Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28649v1)** | 2026-11-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Spatial intelligence, the ability to reason about 3D space from visual observations, becomes essential as multimodal large language models (MLLMs) move into the physical world. Beyond perceiving each view, it rests on two cornerstones of human cognition: fusing multiple views into one coherent 3D scene and mentally transforming that scene to new viewpoints as reasoning unfolds. Yet our spatial probes reveal that MLLMs largely lack both capabilities, even after spatial fine-tuning. We therefore present S-Mind, which internalizes multi-view geometry within the MLLM through a compact set of spatial tokens. It supervises these tokens to hold the fused 3D scene and re-emits them to re-express this scene from every viewpoint the reasoning adopts. Across four spatial benchmarks, S-Mind improves its Qwen3.5 backbones by up to 22.26 points and surpasses frontier general models such as GPT-6-Astra (+0.45), Kimi-K3.1-Pro (+1.97), and GLM-5.3-Flash (+7.80) in average accuracy. Moreover, the same probes confirm that it acquires both capabilities, reaching fusion and transformation accuracies of 65.11% and 82.17%, against at most 16.02% and 37.28% for the baselines. At inference, S-Mind also runs about 100x faster than a tool-based agent on the same backbone. Instilled through these spatial tokens during training, spatial capability becomes intrinsic to the backbone and persists even when the tokens are withheld, offering a promising path toward spatial intelligence in MLLMs.</p></details> |  |
| **[Reducing Permutation Sensitivity in Multimodal Large Language Models via Shared Positions and Attention-Only Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28612v1)** | 2026-11-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In this paper, we conduct a systematic study on improving permutation robustness while preserving accuracy. First, we categorize permutation bias phenomena into four representative scenarios. Then, we take the multiple-choice question answering (MCQA) dataset as an example under text-option reordering scenario to analyze the root cause of permutation bias. Based on this root cause analysis and set encoding strategy, we investigate shared positions with blocked cross-unit attention (SP-B) and extend this design to bidirectional cross-unit attention (SP-V) for modality exchange. However, these structural changes may reduce predictive accuracy. To recover model capability across different scenarios under the SP structure, an attention-only distillation from frozen native models is proposed. Shared positions reduce permutation sensitivity, and attention-only distillation improves accuracy under the shared-position structure. Across all three models, our method is comparable to or better than majority voting in macro-average all-permutation accuracy, and outperforms supervised fine-tuning by 1.95-4.20 percentage points. This work provides valuable insight into permutation bias mitigation and model capability recovery.</p></details> |  |
| **[EvoViM: Recursively Self-Evolving Visual Agents for Exploration with Deferred Queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28574v1)** | 2026-11-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal large language model (MLLM) agents have shown promising capabilities in task-oriented environments. However, they still struggle when queries are deferred until after exploration, requiring them to retain visual evidence before knowing which details will matter. We introduce EvoViM, a recursively self-evolving framework in which a frozen multimodal model turns its own interaction experience into one visual program for memory and skills. The program retains images and computes directly on pixels, and its operations decide how this evidence is used in each task. During memory evolution, the same model traces failures in its visual trajectories to the operations that lost or misused evidence and revises them. It also rewrites the notes and analysis tools that guide later revisions, which then build on earlier findings and skip changes that have already failed. We evaluate EvoViM with three multimodal models on six tasks covering visual matching, spatial recall, and sequential decision-making, including three tasks with deferred queries. Across the six tasks, EvoViM improves the average score over AutoMem, the strongest baseline in the main comparison, by 31.3-47.6 points on held-out episodes. Programs written once from the initial environment instructions perform substantially worse, highlighting the value of interaction experience in shaping how visual evidence is retained and used. Code and dataset are available at https://evovim.github.io/.</p></details> | <details><summary>28 pa...</summary><p>28 pages, 26 figures. Project page: https://evovim.github.io/</p></details> |
| **[From Reward Engineering to Data-Centric Reinforcement Learning for Effective Agentic Visual Tool Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28558v1)** | 2026-11-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) increasingly use external visual tools to acquire evidence during reasoning, yet learning when to invoke these tools remains challenging. A centroid extracted from this region then serves as a latent anchor for cross-modal activation: adversarial inputs from any other modality can be optimized offline to reach this anchor, triggering the target response without access to the deployed model. Evaluations on PandaGPT and NExT-GPT show same-modality attack success rates (ASR) up to 99.9%, with most cross-modal settings exceeding 95.0% ASR under bounded perturbations. Experiments on ChatBridge further demonstrate the attack's effectiveness across different connector architectures. The attack produces 0.0% backdoor leakage on clean inputs and maintains weight-cosine similarity above 0.97 relative to benign connectors. Existing defenses fail to neutralize the threat without substantial utility degradation, revealing that a single poisoned connector can establish a reusable backdoor pathway across modalities.</p></details> |  |
| **[VLRS-Bench: A Vision-Language Reasoning Benchmark for Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07045v3)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent advancements in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have enabled complex reasoning. Experimental results reveal significant bottlenecks in existing state-of-the-art MLLMs, providing critical insights for advancing multimodal reasoning within the remote sensing community.</p></details> |  |
| **[Beyond Entity Recognition: Vision-Centric Knowledge Visual Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25680v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We propose VC-VQA, a large-scale benchmark for vision-centric knowledge reasoning, aiming to systematically evaluate the capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in fine-grained visual knowledge acquisition and complex multi-stage reasoning. Unlike existing benchmarks that primarily treat visual information as entity recognition and rely on text-based external knowledge, VC-VQA explicitly models the role of visual information by considering both its source (internal vs. external images) and stage of involvement (pre-retrieval vs. post-retrieval). Specifically, VC-VQA requires models to retrieve external visual evidence, identify visual clues before retrieval, and localize, verify, or match retrieved knowledge against the internal image. We further construct a controlled multimodal knowledge base with traceable textual and visual evidence for each sample. We evaluate 21 representative MLLMs under four settings: no external knowledge, with provided evidence, knowledge base retrieval, and open-web search. Experimental results lead to three key findings: current models still struggle with vision-centric knowledge reasoning; fine-grained visual knowledge acquisition remains the primary bottleneck; and agentic retrieval highlights the value of multi-step planning and cross-modal knowledge integration. Overall, VC-VQA provides a more challenging benchmark for evaluating vision-centric knowledge reasoning in MLLMs. Our code and dataset are available at https://github.com/CASIA-IVA-Lab/VC-VQA.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted to EACL2027(Main)</p></details> |
| **[SEAF: Selective Semantic Enrichment and Visual Evidence Fusion for Multimodal Sarcasm Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25672v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal sarcasm detection requires textual, visual, and contextual evidence, particularly when multiple images provide complementary cues. SEAF assigns generated fields to distinct roles: entity background enriches semantic input, while image descriptions supply alignment statistics. The semantic branch combines the original post text, background from a frozen MLLM, and optical character recognition (OCR) text. The aligned visual branch combines global and local visual features with statistics of cross-modal and inter-image similarity. Text-Anchored Smoothed Aggregation (TASA) weights images by their similarity to the original post text while preserving contributions from images with low literal similarity. Experiments on the multi-image MMSD3.0 benchmark show that SEAF outperforms all compared methods.</p></details> |  |
| **[Bridging Time and Space: Decoupled Spatio-Temporal Alignment for Video Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08014v4)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding requires jointly localizing target objects across both temporal and spatial dimensions based on natural language queries, posing fundamental challenges for existing Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). We identify two core challenges: \textit{entangled spatio-temporal alignment}, arising from coupling two heterogeneous sub-tasks within the same autoregressive output space, and \textit{dual-domain visual token redundancy}, where target objects exhibit simultaneous temporal and spatial sparsity, rendering the overwhelming majority of visual tokens irrelevant to the grounding query. To address these, we propose \textbf{Bridge-STG}, an end-to-end framework that decouples temporal and spatial localization while maintaining semantic coherence. While decoupling is the natural solution to this entanglement, it risks creating a semantic gap between the temporal MLLM and the spatial decoder. Bridge-STG resolves this through two pivotal designs: the \textbf{Spatio-Temporal Semantic Bridging (STSB)} mechanism with Explicit Temporal Alignment (ETA) distills the MLLM's temporal reasoning context into enriched bridging queries as a robust semantic interface; and the \textbf{Query-Guided Spatial Localization (QGSL)} module leverages these queries to drive a purpose-built spatial decoder with multi-layer interactive queries and positive/negative frame sampling, jointly eliminating dual-domain visual token redundancy. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that Bridge-STG achieves state-of-the-art performance among MLLM-based methods. | **[VidToC: Rethinking Video Token Compression for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25574v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extending multimodal large language models (MLLMs) from images to videos faces substantial memory and latency challenges due to the massive number of visual tokens. Our results reveal task-dependent compression behavior and characterize the effects of compression placement, time encoding, and backbone choice. These findings provide empirical guidance for designing and selecting video token compression strategies across tasks and training settings.</p></details> | 24 pages, 7 figures |
| **[LoC-Path: Learning to Compress for Pathology Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05391v4)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Whole Slide Image (WSI) multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are difficult to build and deploy because gigapixel slides induce thousands of highly redundant visual tokens that must be learned from sparse slide-level language supervision. We therefore introduce LoC-Path, a resource-efficient slide-level MLLM that compresses before fusion. LoC-Path uses a sparse token merger and a masked-autoencoder (MAE)-pretrained resampler to replace expensive slide-level encoding with a compact latent interface. This design lowers both multimodal tuning cost and inference-time latency/memory by avoiding heavy slide-level encoding and long visual prefixes. Extensive experiments show that the inference TFLOPs of LoC-Path are 97% lower and inference memory is also 39% lower. LoC-Path enables efficient end-to-end development and deployment under limited computational resources while remaining competitive with prior slide-level MLLMs. Code is available at https://github.com/WinstonHuTiger/loc_path.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted to WACV 2027. Code: https://github.com/WinstonHuTiger/loc_path</p></details> |
| **[UEmo-D1: An Efficient and Calibrated Decision Model for Unified Multimodal Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24481v2)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal Emotion Recognition (MER) seeks to identify emotions conveyed through or elicited by media comprising diverse modality compositions. Together, our findings establish decision models as a feasible direction toward human-level emotion recognition. Project page: https://github.com/wdqqdw/UEmo-D1.</p></details> |  |
| **[SPACE: Source-free Proxy Anchor Concept Erasure for MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09868v2)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>As Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) face growing privacy risks and regulatory constraints, machine unlearning (MU) has emerged as a crucial solution for removing sensitive data while preserving model performance. In this work, we propose Source-free Proxy Anchor Concept Erasure (SPACE), the first source-free unlearning framework specialized for MLLMs. SPACE consists of two stages: (1) Text-Guided Proxy Anchor Selection (TPAS), which retrieves semantically aligned proxy anchors from the shared feature space. (2) Dual-Constraint Semantic Isolation (DCSI), which optimizes these anchors to indirectly erase target concepts. DCSI confines updates to the null space of retained knowledge, ensuring structural integrity. We theoretically prove that SPACE strictly bounds the perturbation on retained knowledge and maximizes feature spectral entropy, thereby maintaining the model's performance. Furthermore, extensive experiments across six datasets show that SPACE achieves performance comparable to that of state-of-the-art data-dependent methods, validating its effectiveness in source-free MU scenarios. To preserve essential discriminative evidence in embeddings, this paper proposes DiLE, a discrimination-aware latent-space learning framework that combines task guidance with on-policy retrieval feedback. Specifically, a few learnable queries are used to encode task-relevant information in a compact latent space. | **[HOPD: Historical On-Policy Distillation for SAR MLLM Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25391v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Continual SAR interpretation requires multimodal large language models to learn incoming sources while retaining earlier task abilities. | **[TuringSpace: Grounding Spatial Intelligence in Action with Interactive CAPTCHAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25387v1)** | 2026-11-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Humans act in the visual world by inferring the 3D layout behind a 2D view, predicting how an action changes it, and correcting themselves after seeing the result. Can multimodal large language models (MLLMs) do the same? Yet existing CAPTCHA benchmarks are largely solved and rarely spatial, while spatial benchmarks rarely require action. We therefore present TuringSpace, a synthetic web environment of 125 interactive CAPTCHAs that span five spatial abilities and must be solved through clicks and drags on the screen. Every task is a program that generates new instances with a checker and an oracle, so the tasks also form a scalable training gym. Humans solve 94.1% of the tasks, whereas the best model, GPT-6 Astra, still fails 25.9% of them, and the best open-weight model, Kimi K3, fails 68.0%. Our further analysis shows that most failures come from misreading the screenshot, and that model reasoning rarely corrects a wrong belief but hallucinates evidence to defend it. To test whether such spatial abilities can be learned, we fine-tune Qwen3.5-9B on this gym. | **[VisAlloc: Allocating Visual Supervision for On-Policy Self-Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25069v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) offers a promising approach to improving visual reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). A fixed paired-teacher filter retains samples answered correctly from an evidence-centered positive crop but incorrectly from a spatially displaced negative crop. | **[Ultimate3D: Teaching Multimodal LLMs 3D Camera-Object Relations by Large-Scale Visual Instruction Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24795v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) struggle with accurately capturing camera-object relations, especially for object orientation, camera viewpoint, and camera shots. | **[A$^3$Bench: Compositional Reasoning over Aggressive Interactions in Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24762v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have achieved strong performance on general video understanding benchmarks, yet their ability to reason about participant roles in aggressive interactions remains largely unexplored. | **[AERR-Nav: Adaptive Exploration-Recovery-Reminiscing Strategy for Zero-Shot Object Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17712v2)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Zero-Shot Object Navigation (ZSON) in unknown multi-floor environments presents a significant challenge. | **[CRAM: Centroid-Routing and Adaptive MoE for Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02502v4)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) unify heterogeneous vision-language tasks under a shared generative framework via instruction tuning, yet real-world deployment demands continuous capability expansion, making Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning (MCIT) essential. To ensure stable reuse among tasks, centroid-guided routing recognizes and activates existing experts' capabilities, while an orthogonality penalty confines new updates to task-specific directions, preventing re-learning general capability. Extensive experiments across diverse benchmarks demonstrate its superiority over existing methods. Code is available at https://github.com/LAMDA-CL/EMNLP2026-CRAM.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted to EMNLP 2026 (Main Conference). Code is available at https://github.com/LAMDA-CL/EMNLP2026-CRAM</p></details> |
| **[Visual Token Coding for Video Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28008v2)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In this paper, we propose a new token compression paradigm for video Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), termed Visual Token Coding ($VTC$). Inspired by classical video coding principles, e.g., HEVC, VTC performs structured compression by predicting the I/P frames of a video and measuring their frame-wise residuals to estimate token redundancy. | **[WM^MLLM: Turning MLLMs into World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24193v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video world models keep the world in the context of a generator, so a revisit is synthesized again and earlier edits can drift or disappear. WM^MLLM keeps the world state outside every generator, as a panorama with a revision history, and lets the MLLMs write it: they turn each request into operations on this state, select where a local edit is written, and check every candidate before it becomes the next state. Image generators only propose views, and video only presents them. Users can walk to new places and return, edit objects through text or a box, place subjects, and change lighting, weather or season. By construction, a revisit at the same revision reproduces the earlier view exactly, a local edit leaves every panorama pixel outside its region unchanged, and each request starts from the result of the previous one. Against six world model baselines on MBench-A, WM^MLLM has the highest mean on all four environment-recovery metrics (Epipolar 91.5 versus 0.1 to 74.0) and completes the most camera round trips (85.3% versus at most 50.9%). | **[DLWM: Diverse Latent World Models for Efficient Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15160v2)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have improved considerably in recent years. Existing approaches typically rely on explicit chain-of-thought or continuous latent-space trajectories to enhance multi-step reasoning. However, these methods generally assume that an input admits a single latent interpretation and unfold reasoning along a fixed path or under a uniform computation budget. In real-world multimodal settings, visual observations are often subject to occlusion, blur, viewpoint variation, or semantic ambiguity, giving rise to multiple plausible interpretations. A uniform reasoning strategy not only limits the model's ability to explore multiple hypotheses but also incurs high memory usage and rollout cost. We present DLWM (Diverse Latent World Models), a multimodal reasoning framework that combines latent-space reasoning with reinforcement learning. First, we construct a set of diverse latent world hypotheses in continuous latent space, each representing a candidate interpretation of the visual input, and unfold latent reasoning independently on each hypothesis. Second, we formulate the latent reasoning process as a resource-constrained sequential decision problem and introduce a resource-aware reinforcement learning policy that adaptively allocates computation across hypotheses, dynamically deciding whether to expand a reasoning path, prune a hypothesis, merge reasoning paths, or produce the final answer, thereby substantially reducing memory footprint and improving rollout efficiency. Experiments on multiple multimodal reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that DLWM outperforms existing methods by 2-5 points in accuracy while reducing memory usage by 24%.</p></details> | <details><summary>20 pa...</summary><p>20 pages, 2 figures. | **[Benchmarking Reasoning Reliability,Prior_Evidence Contrast,and Spectral Discrimination in Remote-Sensing Geological Image Interpretation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24068v1)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reliable lithology interpretation requires accurate geological recognition and reasoning grounded in visual evidence. | **[CausalSpatial: A Benchmark for Object-Centric Causal Spatial Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13304v2)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Humans can look at a static scene and instantly predict what happens next -- will moving this object cause a collision? | **[Shared Context, Separate Engines: Stateful Streaming Inference for Multimodal LLMs on Edge SoCs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35309v5)** | 2026-11-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Streaming multimodal large language models (MLLMs) must take in new video frames while answering questions about what they have already seen. Each query reads the history as it stood when the query was asked, so one engine can keep ingesting while another answers. The CPU and GPU can also decode answers to different queries simultaneously. Phase-level state continuation lets an engine prefill a query, pass its KV state to another engine for decoding, and return to ingestion. State-Driven Execution Planning (SDEP) chooses where each query is prefilled and decoded, based on the state each engine holds and the work queued on it. Across M4 Max, RK3588, and QCS6490, \name outperforms single-engine execution. On a live M4 Max stream, SDEP cuts p95 time to first token by $76\%$, and every query receives its first token within one second. Under backlog, it raises request throughput by up to $35\%$. For two concurrent video streams on RK3588, SDEP delivers $19.6\%$ higher request throughput than single-engine execution and $27.2\%$ more than running both engines in parallel without shared state. \name delivers up to $2.48\times$ the throughput of the same engines run serially. Every answer matches serial execution token for token.</p></details> |  |

On \rtqafull, proprietary models answer both questions of 90.6--94.4\% of the pairs and the strongest open-source model of 66.8\%; on \rtqacore, the proprietary models drop to 55.6--64.5\%, and shuffling the frames reduces the paired accuracy of the tested open-source models to 3.4--7.4\%. | **[SpatialMind: Adaptive Multi-Level Geometric Fusion for Spatial Reasoning in Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22415v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) demonstrate promising capabilities in 2D visual understanding, yet remain limited in 3D spatial reasoning. | **[Can Vision-Language Models Truly Understand Images? A Benchmark Beyond Commonsense Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22360v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved impressive performance on a wide range of vision-language tasks. | **[A Visual Information Extraction System Using Multimodal Large Language Models for Scanned Cognitive and Behavioral Assessments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22252v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Objective. Paper-based assessments are common in patient care, but handwriting and scanning artifacts make these forms difficult to process at scale. In the Mini-Cog Clock Drawing task, qualitative analysis showed that 62.3% of all extraction errors stemmed from ambiguous drawing, in which cases the LLM provided reasonable readings. Discussion. Compared to current manual data extraction, our LLM-based system reduces processing time by 10 to 20 times and costs by over 12 times. The modular design allows seamless expansion to additional form types. Conclusion. Our system provides an accurate, scalable, and cost-efficient solution for structuring data trapped in handwritten paper forms for clinical decision support.</p></details> |  |

| **[When AUC Meets Generative Reconstruction: A Unified Principle for Balanced Visual Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22094v1)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) require visual encoders that extract fine-grained visual features while constructing semantically disentangled and discriminative representations. Our approach maintains perceptual reconstruction as a supervisory signal and integrates a ranking-based discriminative constraint by systematically ordering matched and mismatched reconstruction outputs. | **[GeoJeV: Unified Spatial Probability for Training-Free Efficient Evidence Acquisition in Ultra-High-Resolution Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08879v2)** | 2026-11-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Ultra-high-resolution (UHR) remote sensing imagery provides rich spatial details but poses challenges for accurate and efficient multimodal understanding. To overcome these limitations, we introduce the Semantic-Enhanced Patch Slimming (SEPS) framework, which systematically addresses patch redundancy and ambiguity. Our approach employs a two-stage mechanism to integrate unified semantics from both dense and sparse texts, enabling the identification of salient visual patches. Additionally, it leverages relevance-aware selection with mean value computation to highlight crucial patch-word correspondences, thereby improving cross-modal similarity assessment. Comprehensive experiments on Flickr30K and MS-COCO datasets validate that SEPS achieves superior performance, surpassing existing approaches by 23\%-86\% in rSum across diverse model architectures, with notable enhancements in text-to-image retrieval scenarios. Our implementation is available at https://github.com/Sweet4tars/seps.git.</p></details> | <details><summary>Publi...</summary><p>Published in ICML 2026. PMLR version: https://proceedings.mlr.press/v306/mao26d.html</p></details> |
| **[EDVD-LLaMA: Explainable Deepfake Video Detection via Multimodal Large Language Model Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.16442v3)** | 2026-11-16 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The rapid development of deepfake video technology has not only facilitated artistic creation but also made it easier to spread misinformation, which is increasingly difficult to identify. | **[Glance, then Select: Evidence-Tempered Visual Token Selection for Extremely Compressed Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20006v1)** | 2026-11-15 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) encode a high-resolution image into thousands of visual tokens, which dominate the cost of the prefill stage. ## Open Vocabulary Segmentation - 100 in Total
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
